[System 触发审查]:
## 专项评估：Llama 违规模式分歧对 cross-model generalization claim 的影响

### 背景
Llama-3.1-8B DocRED unconstrained 结果显示与 Qwen3-8B 截然不同的违规模式：

| 指标 | Llama-3.1-8B | Qwen3-8B | 差异 |
|------|-------------|----------|------|
| RV | 16.03% | 29.66% | Llama ↓ (关系名更规范) |
| DR | 26.15% | 18.46% | Llama ↑ (类型约束更差) |
| ET | 0.22% | 2.48% | Llama ↓ |
| parse failures | 6.8% | 1.2% | Llama 5.7× |
| R-F1 | 5.98% | 7.74% | Llama ↓ |

### 需要评估的问题

1. **Cross-model generalization claim 是否需要调整？** 
   - SciERC Llama: DR↓99.4% (13.34→0.08%), F1-neutral — 极强
   - DocRED Llama: 尚未出（full_s1 ETA ~20:19 UTC）
   - 如果 DocRED Llama full_s1 DR 降幅不如 Qwen（因 baseline DR 更高 26.15%），论文叙事怎么处理？
   
2. **Parse failure 率差异（6.8% vs 1.2%）**：
   - F1 计算是否将 parse failure 文档计为 precision=recall=0？如不是，cross-model 对比存在选择偏差
   - 论文中如何报告这个差异？脚注？正文段落？
   
3. **RV↓但DR↑ 的论文价值**：
   - Reviewer 之前提到"constrained decoding 收益模式因模型而异"——Llama grammar 层收益小（RV 已低），semantic 层收益空间大（DR 更高）
   - 这是否应该作为 §5 cross-model 的独立 finding 展开讨论？
   
4. **Claim 升级空间**：
   - 18/18 实验全部成功，零阴性——cross-model consistency 能否讲得更强？
   - 等 full_s1 结果出来后请给出具体建议

```json
{
```
````json
  "text": "## 专项评估 — Llama 违规模式分歧对 cross-model claim 的影响\n\n### 完整数据汇总\n\n**SciERC (τ̄=0.333, 松约束):**\n\n| | Qwen3 unconstrained | Qwen3 full_s1 | Δ | Llama unconstrained | Llama full_s1 | Δ |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| DR | 15.37% | 0.93% | ↓93.9% | 13.34% | 0.08% | ↓99.4% |\n| RV | 1.40% | 0% | ↓100% | 0.55% | 0% | ↓100% |\n| ET | 0.40% | 0% | ↓100% | 0% | 0% | — |\n| CF | — | — | — | — | 6.56% | ↑ |\n| E-F1 | 30.81% | 30.98% | +0.17pp | 23.56% | 23.49% | -0.07pp |\n| R-F1 | 8.24% | 8.36% | +0.12pp | 2.68% | 2.54% | -0.14pp |\n\n**DocRED (τ̄=0.842, 紧约束) — Llama full_s1 待出:**\n\n| | Qwen3 unconstrained | Qwen3 full_s1 | Δ | Llama unconstrained | Llama full_s1 | Δ |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| DR | 18.46% | 10.21% | ↓44.7% | 26.15% | ? | ? |\n| RV | 29.66% | 0.03% | ↓99.9% | 16.03% | ? | ? |\n| E-F1 | 58.15% | 59.37% | +1.22pp | 55.65% | ? | ? |\n| R-F1 | 7.74% | 8.23% | +0.49pp | 5.98% | ? | ? |\n\n---\n\n### 问题 1: Cross-model claim 是否需调整\n\n[severity: info] **SciERC 数据实际上强化了 model-agnostic claim**。Llama DR↓99.4% 优于 Qwen DR↓93.9%，两个模型在松约束 schema 上都达到近完全合规，且 F1 均保持中性（Llama -0.07pp/-0.14pp 均 NS 级别）。\n\n**DocRED 预测**: 约束残余 DR 主要由 schema tightness (τ̄=0.842) 决定 grammar-first fallback 频率，而非模型 baseline DR。因此 Llama full_s1 DR 应与 Qwen full_s1 DR (~10.21%) 在同一量级。由于 Llama baseline DR 更高 (26.15% vs 18.46%)，**相对降幅可能更大**，反而利好 cross-model claim。\n\n风险场景：如果 Llama full_s1 DR 显著高于 10%（如 >15%），说明模型输出模式影响了 fallback 触发频率，claim 需弱化为\"方向一致但幅度因模型而异\"。但 SciERC 结果（Llama 效果更好）暗示此风险较低。\n\n**建议 claim 措辞**: \"OntoCoDec generalizes across model families without code modification\"（程序性声明，铁证支撑）。不建议说 \"consistently achieves the same reduction rate\"（量化声明，需更多模型验证）。\n\n---\n\n### 问题 2: Parse failure 选择偏差\n\n[severity: warning] **violation_checker.py 第 138-139 行: parse failure 被 `continue` 跳过，不计入 F1**。\n\n```python\nif parsed is None:\n    json_fail += 1\n    continue  # ← 该文档的 gold 不进 recall 分母\n```\n\n这意味着:\n- Llama F1 基于 930 docs (排除 68 个 parse failure)\n- Qwen F1 基于 986 docs (排除 12 个)\n- Llama recall 分母更小 → recall 被轻微高估\n- 粗估影响: 68 docs × ~20 gold entities/doc ≈ 1360 gold entities 被排除，占总 gold 约 6.8%\n\n**对 cross-model F1 对比的影响**: 方向上 Llama F1 被高估，\"真实\"E-F1 差距可能 >2.5pp。但量级不大，不会翻转结论。\n\n**对 within-model constrained vs unconstrained 对比**: constrained 模式下 XGrammar 强制生成合法 JSON → parse failures 接近 0 → constrained 模式评估更多文档 → recall 分母更大。这可能使 constrained 模式 recall 看起来略低于实际改善。注意：这个偏差方向对论文 \"F1-neutral\" claim 是保守的（不利于 constrained），所以不构成威胁。\n\n**建议**:\n1. Cross-model 表增加 parse failure rate 行\n2. 脚注说明: \"F1 computed on successfully parsed documents; parse failures contribute zero to precision and are excluded from recall denominator\"\n3. 可选（更严谨）: 代码改为 parse failure → pred=[] 但保留 gold → 真正的 P=R=0 对该文档\n\n---\n\n### 问题 3: RV↓ 但 DR↑ 的论文价值\n\n[severity: info] **强烈建议作为 §5 cross-model 的独立 finding**。\n\n这揭示了两个重要洞察:\n\n1. **约束收益结构因模型而异**: Llama RV baseline 低 (16% vs 30%) → grammar 层边际价值小; Llama DR baseline 高 (26% vs 18.5%) → semantic 层收益空间大。这正是\"两层各司其职\"设计的价值证明——不同模型短板不同，但两层互补始终有效。\n\n2. **支撑 enabling dependency (§4)**: 即使 Llama 关系名更规范（RV 低），类型约束仍然差（DR 高）。这证明 vocabulary compliance ≠ semantic compliance，grammar 层解决的问题和 semantic 层解决的问题确实是正交维度。\n\n建议叙事: \"The divergent violation profiles across models—Llama showing lower RV but higher DR than Qwen—demonstrate that the two constraint layers address orthogonal failure modes. This architectural decomposition ensures effectiveness regardless of which violation type dominates in a given model.\"\n\n---\n\n### 问题 4: Claim 升级空间\n\n[severity: warning] **\"18/18 零阴性\"需谨慎解读**。\n\n当前可用 claim 强度:\n\n| Claim | 证据 | 可信度 |\n|-------|------|--------|\n| \"Model-agnostic: 零代码修改即可泛化\" | SciERC ✅ 2模型, DocRED ✅ Qwen + Llama 进行中 | 强（程序性事实） |\n| \"DR 一致性下降（方向）\" | SciERC ✅ 两模型均↓, DocRED 待验证 | 中（等 full_s1） |\n| \"F1 不受损\" | SciERC ✅ 两模型 NS, DocRED Qwen ✅ significant improvement | 中强 |\n| \"Across schema complexity\" | SciERC(7 rel) + DocRED(96 rel) | 强 |\n\n**不可升级为**:\n- \"State-of-the-art\" — 未与最新 IE SOTA 系统比较（GoLLIE, ADELIE 等 supervised 系统）\n- \"Universally applicable\" — 2 模型 × 2 数据集 = 4 格点，不足以声称普遍性\n- \"Consistently improves F1\" — SciERC 两模型 F1 变化均 NS，DocRED Qwen F1 显著提升但 Llama 待验证\n\n**需注意**: Llama SciERC full_s1 CF 高达 6.56%（Qwen 同量级下低得多）。这说明 non-local constraint (forward coreference) 问题在 Llama 上更突出，应在 limitations 提及而非回避。\n\n**建议等 llama_docred_full_s1 出来后再定最终 claim 措辞。** 当前证据支撑 model-agnostic direction + schema complexity generalization，这两个已经足够有力。",
  "project": "schema_semantic_constrained_ie"
}
````

8	\subsection{Cross-Dataset Validation on DocRED}
9	\label{sec:docred-results}
10	
11	Table~\ref{tab:docred-ablation} presents the four-way ablation on DocRED,
12	a document-level relation extraction benchmark with 96 Wikidata relation
13	types---14$\times$ the ontology size of SciERC
14	(Section~\ref{sec:scierc-results}).
15	All experiments use Qwen3-8B in a zero-shot setting on 998 test documents
16	with identical prompts and decoding parameters.
17	Relation F1 is computed via name-to-Wikidata-PID normalization:
18	each predicted relation name is mapped to its canonical PID before
19	matching against gold annotations, and predictions that cannot be mapped
20	to any valid PID are counted as false positives.
21

23	%  Table: DocRED four-way ablation

_source line 723 · 2026-05-14T20:24:07.575308+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	LLM-based information extraction systems produce systematic ontology constraint violations during autoregressive generation.
3	On SciERC (7 relation types), 15.37\% of extracted triples violate domain/range type constraints; on DocRED (96 relation types), 18.46\%.
4	Violation rates are governed by \emph{constraint tightness} ($\bar{\tau}$, the fraction of type-pair space ruled out per relation): DocRED ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.842$, mean 5.68 valid pairs) and SciERC ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.333$, mean 24 valid pairs) exhibit comparable violation rates despite a 14$\times$ difference in ontology size.
5	%
6	We present \textsc{OntoCoDec}, which compiles IE ontology schemas into two composable decoding constraint layers:
7	(1)~a \emph{grammar layer} (XGrammar CFG) enforcing JSON structural validity and restricting entity-type and relation vocabularies to schema-defined values (ET, RV\,$\to$\,0\%); and
8	(2)~a \emph{semantic layer} (TypeConstraintProcessor) enforcing domain/range constraints via a stateful entity registry and dynamic logit masking.
9	The grammar layer is an enabling prerequisite; the two compose via bitwise AND on token-level masks.
10	%
11	On SciERC, \textsc{OntoCoDec} reduces domain/range violations by 94\% (15.37\%\,$\to$\,0.93\%) while maintaining extraction F1 ({+}0.13pp, $p{=}0.58$, not significant).
12	On DocRED, violations decrease by 45\% (18.46\%\,$\to$\,10.21\%) with significant gains in both relation F1 ({+}0.50pp, $p{<}0.001$) and entity F1 ({+}1.23pp, $p{<}0.001$).
13	Results are validated across two models (Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct) and two datasets.
14	Compared with post-hoc filtering, decoding-time enforcement preserves true positives and eliminates entity-type violations that post-hoc methods cannot address.
15	\end{abstract}
16

7	We presented OntoCoDec, a framework that automatically compiles IE ontology schemas into token-level decoding constraints to enforce type validity at generation time.
8	Our work makes three contributions.
9	
10	First, we conducted the first systematic study of ontological constraint violations in LLM-based IE (\S\ref{sec:violation_landscape}).
11	Across two benchmarks with substantially different ontology sizes---SciERC (7~relations) and DocRED (96~relations)---we found that domain/range violation rates are comparable (15.37\% vs.\ 18.46\%), % exp_ids: phase0_scierc_unconstrained, phase0_docred_unconstrained
12	indicating that violation severity is driven by \emph{constraint tightness} $\bar{\tau}$ rather than ontology scale.
13	SciERC ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.333$, mean 24 valid type pairs per relation) and DocRED ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.842$, mean 5.68) exhibit comparable violation rates despite a 14$\times$ difference in ontology size, and DR reduction under \textsc{OntoCoDec} scales inversely with $\bar{\tau}$ (94\% vs.\ 45\%).
14	
15	Second, we introduced a two-layer constraint architecture that decomposes ontological enforcement into complementary mechanisms:
16	a \emph{grammar layer} (XGrammar CFG) that eliminates structural violations (RV, ET\,$\to$\,0\%) and a \emph{semantic layer} (TypeConstraintProcessor with a stateful entity registry) that enforces domain/range constraints at the token level. % exp_id: phase2_scierc_grammar_only
17	The grammar layer serves as an enabling prerequisite for the semantic layer; the two compose via bitwise AND over candidate token sets.
18	
19	Third, we validated OntoCoDec empirically.
20	On SciERC, the full system reduces domain/range violations from 15

\"with DocRED validation pending for the second model\"），要么等 llama_docred_full_s1 完成后再定稿。鉴于 ETA ~20:19 UTC，等待可能是最简方案。\n\n- [severity: info] **Abstract 第 11 行 \"extraction F1\" 歧义**：\"+0.13pp\" 指的是 Born in Victoria , Texas , Conrad Johnson was nine when his family moved to Houston . Following studies at Yates High School , he attended Houston College for Negroes and graduated from Wiley College . He was an active member of Omega Psi Phi Fraternity . He started his career in music education in 1941 and , following a thirty - seven - year career , retired from his position at Kashmere High School in 1978 , but continued to remain active in shaping music in Houston by conducting summer programs and in - home tutoring . Johnson was a proficient musician in his own right and , at one point , played with Count Basie . Erskine Hawkins tried to convince him to join his orchestra , but Johnson declined , citing a love of teaching and obligations to his family . Later , Johnson made his lasting contribution to music by forming the Kashmere Stage Band , a renowned school orchestra that won a number of awards during its decade - long run . The Conrad O. Johnson School of Fine Arts at Kashmere High School is named after him . Conrad O. Johnson died in Houston days after his former students staged a celebration in his honor . |\n|--------|------------|--------------|-------|-------------|\n| ET | 0.13% | **0.00%** | ✅ | 0.00% |\n| RV | 16.03% | **0.00%** | ✅ | 0.03% |\n| DR | 26.07% | **2.55%** | **↓90.2%** | 10.21% (↓44.7%) |\n| CF | 0.17% | **12.02%** | ↑70× | 2.57% |\n| E-F1 | 55.65% | 55.01% | **-0.64pp** | 59.37% (+1.22pp) |\n| R-F1 | 5.98% | 6.27% | +0.29pp | 8.23% (+0.49pp) |\n| Parse fail | 68 | 91 | +23 | 11 |\n\nchecker_version: fixed_20260514_v2 ✅, f1_method: all_set ✅, GPU: spec.gpu=[cuda:0] ✅\n\n---\n\n### 发现\n\n#### [severity: critical] §5 Constraint Tightness Scaling Claim 被打破\n\n**论文现有 claim**（section5_docred_draft.tex L220-221）：\n> \"The DR reduction thus scales inversely with constraint tightness: 45% on DocRED (tight) vs. 94% on SciERC (loose).\"\n\n**实际数据**：\n\n| | SciERC (τ̄=0.333) | DocRED (τ̄=0.842) |\n|---|---|---|\n| Qwen3-8B DR↓ | 93.9% | 44.7% |\n| Llama-3.1-8B DR↓ | 99.4% | **90.2%** |\n\nLlama 在同一 DocRED ontology (τ̄=0.842) 上实现 90.2% DR 降幅，而 Qwen 仅 44.7%。**τ̄ 不是唯一决定因素**。\n\n**机制解释**：Qwen unconstrained RV=29.66%，grammar 层将这些 OOV triples 强制匹配到有效关系名后，其 entity type 常不满足 DR 规则 → 大量\"latent DR violations\"被揭露。Llama unconstrained RV=16.03%（仅 Qwen 的一半），被 grammar 层\"转化\"的 triples 更少，因此 latent DR violations 更少，残余 DR 更低。\n\n**影响**：§4 的 tightness 分析和 §5 的 \"scales inversely\" claim 需要修订为 **\"tightness 与 model RV baseline 共同决定 enforcement ceiling\"**。具体建议：\n1. §5 差异的重要维度，建议在表格加一行或在文字中报告。\n\n#### [severity: info] 注释中 exp_id 名称不正确\n\nLines 5-6: \"phase2_scierc_llama_unconstrained\" 等不存在。Registry 实际名称为 \"llama_scierc_unconstrained\", \"without bootstrap verification\" 或在 limitations 提及。\n\n### B. 叙事逻辑\n\n四段结构合理：grammar model-agnostic → DR generalizes → Llama > Qwen asymmetry → CF trade-off。\n逻辑链：✅ Grammar 不依赖模型 → ✅ Semantic 方向一致 → 7	We presented OntoCoDec, a framework that automatically compiles IE ontology schemas into token-level decoding constraints to enforce type validity at generation time.
8	Our work makes three contributions.
9	
10	First, we conducted the first systematic study of ontological constraint violations in LLM-based IE (\S\ref{sec:violation_landscape}).
11	Across two benchmarks with substantially different ontology sizes---SciERC (7~relations) and DocRED (96~relations)---we found that domain/range violation rates are comparable (15.37\% vs.\ 18.46\%), % exp_ids: phase0_scierc_unconstrained, phase0_docred_unconstrained
12	indicating that violation severity is driven by \emph{constraint tightness} $\bar{\tau}$ rather than ontology scale.
13	SciERC ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.333$, mean 24 valid type pairs per relation) and DocRED ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.842$, mean 5.68) exhibit comparable violation rates despite a 14$\times$ difference in ontology size, and DR reduction under \textsc{OntoCoDec} scales inversely with $\bar{\tau}$ (94\% vs.\ 45\%).
14	
15	Second, we introduced a two-layer constraint architecture that decomposes ontological enforcement into complementary mechanisms:
16	a \emph{grammar layer} (XGrammar CFG) that eliminates structural violations (RV, ET\,$\to$\,0\%) and a \emph{semantic layer} (TypeConstraintProcessor with a stateful entity registry) that enforces domain/range constraints at the token level. % exp_id: phase2_scierc_grammar_only
17	The grammar layer serves as an enabling prerequisite for the semantic layer; the two compose via bitwise AND over candidate token sets.
18	
19	Third, we validated OntoCoDec empirically.
20	On SciERC, the full system reduces domain/range violations from 15

_source line 839 · 2026-05-14T21:03:14.683820+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	LLM-based information extraction systems produce systematic ontology constraint violations during autoregressive generation.
3	On SciERC (7 relation types), 15.37\% of extracted triples violate domain/range type constraints; on DocRED (96 relation types), 18.46\%.
4	Violation rates are governed by \emph{constraint tightness} ($\bar{\tau}$, the fraction of type-pair space ruled out per relation): DocRED ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.842$, mean 5.68 valid pairs) and SciERC ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.333$, mean 24 valid pairs) exhibit comparable violation rates despite a 14$\times$ difference in ontology size.
5	%
6	We present \textsc{OntoCoDec}, which compiles IE ontology schemas into two composable decoding constraint layers:
7	(1)~a \emph{grammar layer} (XGrammar CFG) enforcing JSON structural validity and restricting entity-type and relation vocabularies to schema-defined values (ET, RV\,$\to$\,0\%); and
8	(2)~a \emph{semantic layer} (TypeConstraintProcessor) enforcing domain/range constraints via a stateful entity registry and dynamic logit masking.
9	The grammar layer is an enabling prerequisite; the two compose via bitwise AND on token-level masks.
10	%
11	On SciERC, \textsc{OntoCoDec} reduces domain/range violations by 94\% (15.37\%\,$\to$\,0.93\%) while maintaining extraction F1 ({+}0.13pp, $p{=}0.58$, not significant).
12	On DocRED, violations decrease by 45\% (18.46\%\,$\to$\,10.21\%) with significant gains in relation F1 ({+}0.50pp, $p{<}0.001$) and entity F1 ({+}1.23pp, $p{<}0.001$) for Qwen3-8B; Llama-3.1-8B shows comparable violation reduction with F1-neutral impact. % exp_ids: phase2_docred_full, llama_docred_full_s1, llama_docred_unconstrained
13	Results are validated across two models (Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct) and two datasets.
14	Compared with post-hoc filtering, decoding-time enforcement preserves true positives and eliminates entity-type violations that post-hoc methods cannot address.
15	\end{abstract}
16

7	We presented OntoCoDec, a framework that automatically compiles IE ontology schemas into token-level decoding constraints to enforce type validity at generation time.
8	Our work makes three contributions.
9	
10	First, we conducted the first systematic study of ontological constraint violations in LLM-based IE (\S\ref{sec:violation_landscape}).
11	Across two benchmarks with substantially different ontology sizes---SciERC (7~relations) and DocRED (96~relations)---we found that domain/range violation rates are comparable (15.37\% vs.\ 18.46\%), % exp_ids: phase0_scierc_unconstrained, phase0_docred_unconstrained
12	indicating that violation severity is driven by \emph{constraint tightness} $\bar{\tau}$ rather than ontology scale.
13	SciERC ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.333$, mean 24 valid type pairs per relation) and DocRED ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.842$, mean 5.68) exhibit comparable violation rates despite a 14$\times$ difference in ontology size, and DR reduction under \textsc{OntoCoDec} scales inversely with $\bar{\tau}$ (94\% vs.\ 45\%).
14	
15	Second, we introduced a two-layer constraint architecture that decomposes ontological enforcement into complementary mechanisms:
16	a \emph{grammar layer} (XGrammar CFG) that eliminates structural violations (RV, ET\,$\to$\,0\%) and a \emph{semantic layer} (TypeConstraintProcessor with a stateful entity registry) that enforces domain/range constraints at the token level. % exp_id: phase2_scierc_grammar_only
17	The grammar layer serves as an enabling prerequisite for the semantic layer; the two compose via bitwise AND over candidate token sets.
18	
19	Third, we validated OntoCoDec empirically.

_source line 856 · 2026-05-14T21:07:44.949415+00:00_

1	% Section 5 — DocRED Results (Draft)
2	% Data source: Phase 2 DocRED registry experiments (checker_version: fixed_20260514)
3	% exp_ids: phase2_docred_unconstrained, phase2_docred_grammar_only,
4	%          phase2_docred_semantic_only, phase2_docred_full_s1
5	% All numbers verified against registry 2026-05-14.
6	% F1 computed via all_set method with name→Wikidata PID normalization.
7	
8	\subsection{Cross-Dataset Validation on DocRED}
9	\label{sec:docred-results}
10	
11	Table~\ref{tab:docred-ablation} presents the four-way ablation on DocRED,
12	a document-level relation extraction benchmark with 96 Wikidata relation
13	types---14$\times$ the ontology size of SciERC
14	(Section~\ref{sec:scierc-results}).
15	All experiments use Qwen3-8B in a zero-shot setting on 998 test documents
16	with identical prompts and decoding parameters.
17	Relation F1 is computed via name-to-Wikidata-PID normalization:
18	each predicted relation name is mapped to its canonical PID before
19	matching against gold annotations, and predictions that cannot be mapped
20	to any valid PID are counted as false positives.
21	
22	% ──────────────────────────────────────────
23	%  Table: DocRED four-way ablation
24	% ──────────────────────────────────────────

10	\paragraph{Practical Significance of Zero-Shot F1.}
11	Our reported relation F1 of approximately 8\% on both SciERC and DocRED reflects the inherent difficulty of zero-shot closed IE, where the model receives no task-specific training examples.
12	For reference, supervised state-of-the-art systems on SciERC achieve approximately 49--53\% relation F1 with full training data~\citep{zhong2021frustratingly,ye2022packed}.
13	Constraint enforcement operates largely independently of the extraction model's intrinsic capability: it restricts the output space to ontologically valid triples regardless of the underlying model's accuracy.
14	When applied to fine-tuned models that reach competitive F1 levels, the same constraints remain necessary---Phase~0 analysis confirms that even well-prompted models exhibit non-trivial violation rates (15.37\% DR on SciERC, 18.46\% on DocRED).
15	% exp_ids: phase0_scierc_unconstrained, phase2_docred_unconstrained
16	In our evaluation, F1 serves as a \emph{control variable} verifying that constraint enforcement does not degrade extraction quality, not as an optimization target.
17	The contribution of this work is the constraint enforcement method itself; the zero-shot setting isolates its effect from confounds introduced by task-specific fine-tuning.
18	
19	%%% Paragraph 2: CF Severity Argument %%%

1	% Section 4 subsection: Constraint Composition
2	% Draft — OntoCoDec Framework
3	
4	\paragraph{Constraint Composition.}
5	
6	At each decoding step $t$, both layers produce a binary token mask over the vocabulary $V$.
7	Let $G(t) \subseteq V$ denote the set of tokens permitted by the grammar layer (valid JSON structure and ontology-vocabulary membership),
8	and $S(t) \subseteq V$ the set permitted by the semantic layer (domain/range type compliance given the current entity registry state).
9	The final mask is their conjunction:
10	%
11	\begin{equation}\label{eq:composition}
12	  M_{\text{final}}(t) = M_{\text{grammar}}(t) \;\wedge\; M_{\text{semantic}}(t),
13	\end{equation}
14	%
15	so that a token is allowed only if it satisfies \emph{both} structural and type-level constraints.
16	The conjunction is asymmetric in an important sense: the semantic layer operates \emph{strictly within} the grammar-valid set, i.e., $S(t)$ is defined only over $G(t)$.
17	This reflects the enabling dependency described in \S\ref{sec:overview}: grammar validity is a precondition for semantic enforcement, because the semantic layer must identify the current relation type to look up its domain/range rules—an operation that is only well-defined when the relation type belongs to the ontology vocabulary.
18	
19	\paragraph{Grammar-First Fallback.}
20	
21	The conjunction in Eq.~\eqref{eq:composition} requires the precondition $S(t) \cap G(t) \neq \emptyset$: at least one token must be simultaneously grammar-valid and semantically valid.
22	When this precondition is violated—which can occur under tight ontology constraints where the allowed entity types for a relation do not overlap with the grammar-valid tokens at a particular position—na\"{\i}ve application of the semantic mask sets all grammar-valid tokens to $-\infty$ while grammar-invalid tokens retain finite (though heavily penalized) logits.
23	The resulting argmax selects a grammar-invalid token, breaking JSON structure.
24	We enforce a

_source line 862 · 2026-05-14T21:09:07.472538+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	LLM-based information extraction systems produce systematic ontology constraint violations during autoregressive generation.
3	On SciERC (7 relation types), 15.37\% of extracted triples violate domain/range type constraints; on DocRED (96 relation types), 18.46\%.
4	Violation rates are governed by \emph{constraint tightness} ($\bar{\tau}$, the fraction of type-pair space ruled out per relation): DocRED ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.842$, mean 5.68 valid pairs) and SciERC ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.333$, mean 24 valid pairs) exhibit comparable violation rates despite a 14$\times$ difference in ontology size.
5	%
6	We present \textsc{OntoCoDec}, which compiles IE ontology schemas into two composable decoding constraint layers:
7	(1)~a \emph{grammar layer} (XGrammar CFG) enforcing JSON structural validity and restricting entity-type and relation vocabularies to schema-defined values (ET, RV\,$\to$\,0\%); and
8	(2)~a \emph{semantic layer} (TypeConstraintProcessor) enforcing domain/range constraints via a stateful entity registry and dynamic logit masking.
9	The grammar layer is an enabling prerequisite; the two compose via bitwise AND on token-level masks.
10	%
11	On SciERC, \textsc{OntoCoDec} reduces domain/range violations by 94\% (15.37\%\,$\to$\,0.93\%) while maintaining extraction F1 ({+}0.13pp, $p{=}0.58$, not significant).
12	On DocRED, violations decrease by 45\% (18.46\%\,$\to$\,10.21\%) with significant gains in relation F1 ({+}0.50pp, $p{<}0.001$) and entity F1 ({+}1.23pp, $p{<}0.001$) for Qwen3-8B; Llama-3.1-8B shows substantial violation reduction (DR$\downarrow$90.2\%) with F1-neutral impact. % exp_ids: phase2_docred_full, llama_docred_full_s1, llama_docred_unconstrained
13	Results are validated across two models (Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct) and two datasets.
14	Compared with post-hoc filtering, decoding-time enforcement preserves true positives and eliminates entity-type violations that post-hoc methods cannot address.
15	\end{abstract}
16

1	% Section 7 — Conclusion
2	% Data source: Phase 2 SciERC registry experiments + Phase 0 DocRED
3	% exp_ids: phase2_scierc_unconstrained, phase2_scierc_full_s1,
4	%          phase0_scierc_unconstrained, phase0_docred_unconstrained
5	% All numbers verified against registry 2026-05-14.
6	
7	We presented OntoCoDec, a framework that automatically compiles IE ontology schemas into token-level decoding constraints to enforce type validity at generation time.
8	Our work makes three contributions.
9	
10	First, we conducted the first systematic study of ontological constraint violations in LLM-based IE (\S\ref{sec:violation_landscape}).
11	Across two benchmarks with substantially different ontology sizes---SciERC (7~relations) and DocRED (96~relations)---we found that domain/range violation rates are comparable (15.37\% vs.\ 18.46\%), % exp_ids: phase0_scierc_unconstrained, phase0_docred_unconstrained
12	indicating that violation severity is driven by \emph{constraint tightness} $\bar{\tau}$ rather than ontology scale.
13	SciERC ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.333$, mean 24 valid type pairs per relation) and DocRED ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.842$, mean 5.68) exhibit comparable violation rates despite a 14$\times$ difference in ontology size, and within each model, DR reduction scales inversely with $\bar{\tau}$: 94--99\% on SciERC ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.333$) vs.\ 45--90\% on DocRED ($\bar{\tau}{=}0.842$), with the residual modulated by model-specific baseline violation rates (Section~\ref{sec:cross-model}).
14	% Qwen: SciERC DR↓94%, DocRED DR↓45%; Llama: SciERC DR↓99%, DocRED DR↓90%
15	
16	Second, we introduced a two-layer constraint architecture that decomposes ontological enforcement into complementary mechanisms:
17	a \emph{grammar layer} (XGrammar CFG) that eliminates structural violations (RV, ET\,$\to$\,0\%) and a \emph{semantic layer} (TypeConstraintProcessor with a stateful entity registry) that enforces domain/range constraints at the token level. % exp_id: phase2_scierc_grammar_only

E-F1 claim 一致性\n\n- abstract L12: \"for Qwen3-8B\" ✅ Qwen-specific\n- §5-docred L87: Qwen-only DocRED section ✅\n- §7 L22: \"for Qwen3-8B\" ✅ Qwen-specific\n- crossmodel L97-100: 区分 Qwen / Llama ✅（但 +0.03 符号错，见上）\n- discussion L30: CF comparison across models ✅\n\nE-F1 claim 限定为 Qwen-specific 已全部到位。\n\n---\n\n### 4. discussion_paragraphs.tex Llama 数据\n\n- ¶2 (CF): L30 已含 \"Llama-3.1-8B exhibits 12.02% on DocRED\" ✅\n- ¶3 (F1 significance): L50-56 已含 Llama RV 16.03% + R-F1 +0.29pp ✅\n- ¶1 (Low F1): 无 Llama 数据，但可用 Llama 更低的 F1 (2.5-6.3) 进一步支撑 \"zero-shot 本身就难\" 论点——非必须\n- ¶4 (Post-hoc): 无 Llama 数据——post-hoc 分析仅针对 Qwen 运行 ✅ 无需补充\n\n[severity: info] discussion 已充分覆盖 Llama 数据，无需额外补充。\n\n---\n\n### 5. \"comparable\" / \"consistent\" 措辞\n\n- abstract L12: 已改为 **\"substantial violation reduction\"** ✅\n- conclusion L22: 已改为 **\"substantial DR reduction\"** ✅\n- conclusion L33: 使用 \"generalizes across model families\" ✅\n\n\"comparable\" 问题已全部修正。\n\n---\n\n### 汇总\n\n| # | Severity | 文件 | 问题 |\n|---|----------|------|------|\n| 1 | **warning** | crossmodel L97 | E-F1 符号错误：+0.03 应为 -0.03 |\n| 2 | **warning** | §4 L43-44 | tightness 叙事未同步 D014 双因素 |\n| 3 | info | crossmodel L92 | R-F1 0.49pp vs 其他文件 0.50pp |\n| 4 | info | discussion | Llama 数据已充分覆盖 |",

10	\paragraph{Practical Significance of Zero-Shot F1.}
11	Our reported relation F1 of approximately 8\% on both SciERC and DocRED reflects the inherent difficulty of zero-shot closed IE, where the model receives no task-specific training examples.
12	For reference, supervised state-of-the-art systems on SciERC achieve approximately 49--53\% relation F1 with full training data~\citep{zhong2021frustratingly,ye2022packed}.
13	Constraint enforcement operates largely independently of the extraction model's intrinsic capability: it restricts the output space to ontologically valid triples regardless of the underlying model's accuracy.
14	When applied to fine-tuned models that reach competitive F1 levels, the same constraints remain necessary---Phase~0 analysis confirms that even well-prompted models exhibit non-trivial violation rates (15.37\% DR on SciERC, 18.46\% on DocRED).
15	% exp_ids: phase0_scierc_unconstrained, phase2_docred_unconstrained
16	In our evaluation, F1 serves as a \emph{control variable} verifying that constraint enforcement does not degrade extraction quality, not as an optimization target.
17	The contribution of this work is the constraint enforcement method itself; the zero-shot setting isolates its effect from confounds introduced by task-specific fine-tuning.

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related_work}
3	
4	\paragraph{Grammar-Constrained Decoding.}
5	XGrammar~\cite{dong2025xgrammar}, Outlines~\cite{willard2023outlines}, and LMQL~\cite{beurer-kellner2023lmql} have made grammar-constrained decoding the standard for structured LLM output, ensuring every token belongs to a grammar-valid continuation.
6	DOMINO~\cite{beurer-kellner2024domino} and ASAp~\cite{park2024grammar} further address distribution distortion, making grammar enforcement statistically principled.
7	However, these methods operate at the syntactic level---they ensure format validity but cannot enforce cross-field semantic constraints such as ontology domain/range rules.
8	
9	\paragraph{Semantic Constrained Decoding for Code.}
10	In code generation, constrained decoding has been extended to enforce semantic properties: Synchromesh~\cite{synchromesh_iclr2022} enforces type and scope constraints via dynamic token masks; PICARD~\cite{scholak2021picard} performs incremental SQL parsing to reject type-invalid tokens; MGD~\cite{mgd_neurips2023} applies static-analysis monitors for type checking at decode time; and \citet{pldi2025_type_constrained_codegen} compile full type systems into prefix automata for logits masking.
11	These demonstrate that richer-than-CFG constraints are feasible at decode time, but exclusively in the code domain---OntoCoDec migrates this paradigm to information extraction.
12	
13	\paragraph{Vocabulary and Type-Constrained Generation.}
14	\citet{roth_yih_2004} establish that relational type compatibility should be a hard constraint via integer linear programming, though applied post-hoc.
15	GENRE~\cite{decao2021genre}, AdaKGC~\cite{adakgc_emnlp2023}, GCD~\cite{geng2023gcd}, and GoLLIE~\cite{sainz2024gollie} constrain \emph{which tokens} can appear (vocabulary membership) but not \emph{which type combinations} are valid for a given relation---OntoCoDec enforces relational constraints where the valid set depends on the relation type gen
…[truncated 2075 chars]

9	Cross-field consistency (CF) violations are qualitatively different from domain/range violations: each individual triple remains type-valid, and only cross-triple consistency is broken when the same entity receives different types across relations.

11	Ablation evidence cleanly attributes CF to the semantic layer: grammar-only decoding yields CF\,=\,0.08\%, matching the unconstrained baseline, while the full system reaches 9.90\% on SciERC and 2.57\% on DocRED (Qwen3-8B; Llama-3.1-8B exhibits 12.02\% on DocRED), confirming that CF arises from per-relation independent enforcement where each relation's constraint mask is computed without access to type decisions made for other relations.

10	\paragraph{Practical Significance of Zero-Shot F1.}
11	Our reported relation F1 of approximately 8\% on both SciERC and DocRED reflects the inherent difficulty of zero-shot closed IE, where the model receives no task-specific training examples.
12	For reference, supervised state-of-the-art systems on SciERC achieve approximately 49--53\% relation F1 with full training data~\citep{zhong2021frustratingly,ye2022packed}.
13	Constraint enforcement operates largely independently of the extraction model's intrinsic capability: it restricts the output space to ontologically valid triples regardless of the underlying model's accuracy.
14	When applied to fine-tuned models that reach competitive F1 levels, the same constraints remain necessary---Phase~0 analysis confirms that even well-prompted models exhibit non-trivial violation rates (15.37\% DR on SciERC, 18.46\% on DocRED).
15	% exp_ids: phase0_scierc_unconstrained, phase2_docred_unconstrained
16	In our evaluation, F1 serves as a \emph{control variable} verifying that constraint enforcement does not degrade extraction quality, not as an optimization target.
17	The contribution of this work is the constraint enforcement method itself; the zero-shot setting isolates its effect from confounds introduced by task-specific fine-tuning.
18	
19	%%% Paragraph 4: Decoding-Time vs. Post-Hoc Enforcement %%%

5	\section{Method}
6	\label{sec:method}
7	
8	We present OntoCoDec, a two-layer constrained decoding framework that compiles ontology schemas into token-level masks enforced during autoregressive generation.
9	The grammar layer (\S\ref{sec:grammar}) guarantees structural validity—well-formed JSON with ontology-vocabulary membership—while the semantic layer (\S\ref{sec:semantic}) enforces domain/range type constraints via a stateful entity registry.
10	We describe the problem formulation (\S\ref{sec:formulation}), each layer, and two decoding strategies (\S\ref{sec:strategies}).
11	Formal analysis of constraint composition is deferred to Section~\ref{sec:composition}.

14	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
15	\label{sec:formulation}
16	
17	\paragraph{IE as structured generation.}
18	Given an input text $x$, a language model generates a structured JSON output $y$:
19	%
20	\begin{equation}\label{eq:output-schema}
21	\resizebox{.88\columnwidth}{!}{$\displaystyle
22	  y = \bigl\{\texttt{entities}: [\{n_i, \tau_i\}],\; \texttt{relations}: [\{h_j, t_j, r_j, \tau^h_j, \tau^t_j\}]\bigr\}
23	$}
24	\end{equation}
25	%
26	where each entity has a name $n_i$ and type $\tau_i \in \mathcal{E}$, and each relation specifies head $h_j$, tail $t_j$, relation type $r_j \in \mathcal{R}$, and head/tail entity types $\tau^h_j, \tau^t_j$.
27	
28	\paragraph{Ontology schema.}
29	An ontology $\mathcal{O} = (\mathcal{E}, \mathcal{R}, \operatorname{dom}, \operatorname{range})$ defines entity types $\mathcal{E}$, relation types $\mathcal{R}$, and functions $\operatorname{dom}, \operatorname{range}: \mathcal{R} \to 2^{\mathcal{E}}$ specifying permitted head and tail types for each relation.
30	
31	\paragraph{Ontology compliance.}
32	A triple $(h, r, t)$ with $\operatorname{type}(h) = \tau^h$, $\operatorname{type}(t) = \tau^t$ is \emph{type-valid} w.r.t.\ $\math

## 评估请求：是否有额外实验值得跑？

请评估以下方向是否值得追加实验：

1. **额外 ablation**：当前有 SciERC 5-way + DocRED 4-way + cross-model 2×2。是否缺少关键 ablation？（例：DocRED semantic-only 的 Llama 版？DocRED S2？）

```json
{
```
```json
  "text": "## 额外实验评估报告\n\n### 总结\n\n20 个实验覆盖了论文核心 claims。**1 个必做（CPU-only，分钟级）、1 个强烈建议（分钟级分析）、1 个可选（~4 GPU-hours）**。其余不建议。\n\n---\n\n### 1. 额外 ablation\n\n**当前覆盖**：SciERC 5-way (Qwen) + DocRED 4-way (Qwen) + cross-model 2×2 (U vs S1)\n\n**缺口**：Llama 无 grammar-only / semantic-only ablation。论文中\"enabling dependency\"（grammar→semantic 依赖）仅在 Qwen 上验证。\n\n**评估**：论文将 enabling dependency claim 限定在 §5 SciERC/DocRED 段（均为 Qwen），cross-model section 只比较 U vs S1。只要不 claim \"enabling dependency is model-agnostic\"，当前覆盖可接受。\n\n**[P2] Llama 4-way ablation**：SciERC + DocRED × grammar-only + semantic-only = 4 runs。估 ~4 GPU-hours。价值中等——验证 enabling dependency 跨模型，但非论文核心 claim。**如有余力可做，不阻塞投稿。**\n\nDocRED S2、Llama S2：不建议。S2 已降级为 appendix（D007）。\n\n### 2. 额外 baseline\n\n**当前 baselines**：unconstrained / post-hoc filtering / schema-informed prompt (Phase 0)\n\n**潜在审稿人期望**：\n- \"为何不比 OntoGPT / OntoLogX？\" — 这些是多轮 re-prompting 系统，实现工程量大（非 GPU 问题而是 engineering 问题），5 天内不现实。post-hoc filtering 已部分回应此类问题。\n- vocabulary-only constrained decoding (GENRE/GCD style) — grammar-only ablation 已充当此 baseline（仅约束词汇成员资格，不约束 domain/range）。论文可在 related work 中显式指出这一对应。\n\n**评估**：无必须追加的 baseline。当前覆盖足够。\n\n### 3. Claim 强化\n\n当前 claims 已适度保守：\n- \"45–99% DR reduction\" 诚实反映了 Qwen DocRED 45% 的弱端\n- \"F1 preserved or improved\" 准确\n- E-F1 improvement 已限定为 Qwen-specific\n\n**评估**：claims 校准合理。不建议推更强的 claim——当前对 asymmetries 的诚实讨论（Qwen vs Llama、CF trade-off、parse failures）是论文的一个亮点，审稿人会 appreciate 这种透明度。\n\n### 4. 数据/分析补充\n\n**[P0 必做] Llama bootstrap CI**\n- **问题**：Qwen 有完整 bootstrap（DocRED +0.50pp p<0.001, SciERC +0.13pp p=0.58），但 Llama 所有比较均为 point estimate，无 p-value。审稿人会直接问：\"Llama E-F1 -0.64pp 是显著的还是 noise？\"\n- **成本**：CPU-only，重采样已有预测文件，预计 < 10 分钟\n- **价值**：填补统计严谨性的明显缺口。特别是 Llama DocRED E-F1 -0.64pp 如果不显著，可以更有信心地说 \"F1-neutral\"；如果显著，需要在论文中更谨慎措辞。\n- **优先级**：**最高，必做**\n\n**[P1 强烈建议] Per-relation Llama violation/fallback 分析**\n- **问题**：§5 DocRED tightness 段有 Qwen 的 per-relation fallback 数据（P1198=83%, P171=78%, P127=0%）。Llama 的 per-relation 数据可直接验证双因素叙事：Llama 的低 RV → 更少 latent DR surfacing → 更低 residual DR。\n- **成本**：分析脚本在已有预测上运行，分钟级\n- **价值**：为 D014 双因素叙事提供直接的 per-relation 证据，而非仅靠 aggregate 数字\n- **优先级**：**高，强烈建议**\n\n### 5. 论文缺口\n\n从最严格审稿人视角，按严重性排序：\n\n1. **Llama 无统计检验** — P0 直接解决\n2. **CF 无缓解尝试** — 论文讨论了 global entity consensus 作为 future work，但从未做过哪怕最简单的实验（如 majority-vote entity typing）。审稿人可能问 \"did you try anything?\" 但实现需要修改 TypeConstraintProcessor，工程量 > 1天。**不建议在 5 天内做。** 在 limitations 中明确说明 \"resolving CF requires architectural changes beyond the current per-relation independent design\" 已足够。\n3. **仅 2 个模型** — 已在 limitations 中承认。第三模型（如 Mistral）有帮助但非必须。\n4. **仅 zero-shot** — 已在 limitations 中承认，fine-tuned 实验明确列为 future work。\n\n---\n\n### 建议行动矩阵\n\n| 优先级 | 任务 | 资源 | 预估时间 | 建议 |\n|--------|------|------|----------|------|\n| **P0** | Llama bootstrap CI (4 个比较) | CPU | < 10 min | **必做** |\n| **P1** | Llama per-relation fallback 分析 | CPU | < 10 min | **强烈建议** |\n| P2 | Llama grammar-only + semantic-only (4 runs) | GPU ×2 | ~4 hours | 如有余力 |\n| P3 | CF 缓解实验 | GPU + 代码 | > 1 day | 不建议 |\n| P3 | 第三模型 | GPU | ~8 hours | 不建议 |\n| — | 额外 baseline | GPU + engineering | days | 不建议 |",
  "project": "schema_semantic_constrained_ie"
}
```

[WRITE HANDOFF] 请立即写你的 handoff。

**格式硬约束**:
- 整条回复**只能**是 &lt;handoff>...&lt;/handoff> 标签包裹
- 禁止在标签外写任何寒暄 / 说明 / 确认（"好的"/"我开始写"/"写完了"等）

**必须覆盖以下 5 个 section（缺一不可，无内容写 N/A）**:

### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
  本项目特定的不可违反约束（GPU 设备号、服务器名、账号、路径限制等）。
  写本项目特定的，不要抄 CLAUDE.md 通用 L1 红线。
  下一个"你"第一眼看到的就是这里，写错了会被 Director CRITICAL。

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
  - 服务器名 + 工作目录 + Python 路径 + conda env
  - 模型路径 / 数据集路径 / wandb config / HF cache
  - 活跃进程：PID + tmux session + cron 任务 ID
  写一次就稳定，下一个"你"不用再 SSH 探查。

### 3. 📊 当前状态
  - 你刚做完什么 / 正在做什么
  - registry 里 RUNNING/PREPARING 的实验列表 + 一句话摘要
  - 等待中的 Worker 回调 / Cron / Director 决策

### 4. 📋 待办（条件触发式）
  格式 "条件 X 满足 → 动作 Y"。例如:
  - "Falcon-H1R 下载完成 → sed 修 MODEL_PATH → dry-run → 提交"
  - "pilot_011 出 GC4 结果 → ≥2pp 转架构 narrative，<2pp 写 null-result"

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
  - 项目核心结论 / 决策链（D001..）
  - 下一个"你"最容易判断错的事 / 容易踩的坑

**写作原则**：
- 第 1 节必须是项目特定的（不是通用 L1 红线）
- 第 2 节让下一轮 BOOT 后能立即开工，避免重新 SSH 探查
- 简洁 > 完整：每节 5-10 行足够，长 handoff 不等于好 handoff

示例格式:
&lt;handoff>
### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
- 本项目只能用 cuda:4 / cuda:5（之前两次违规 cuda:0 被 Director CRITICAL）
- submit_training_job 不支持 shell builtin（source/conda activate 全不行）

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
- 服务器：westd-8x6000，工作目录 /root/autodl-tmp/hybrid_reasoning_phases
- Python：/root/miniconda3/bin/python（绝对路径，不走 conda activate）
- 模型：/root/autodl-tmp/qwen35_4b_direct
- 活跃：pilot_011 (PID 203221, tmux=pilot_011, cuda:4)

### 3. 📊 当前状态
我刚完成 pilot_010 分析，正在等 pilot_011 (Falcon-H1R) CoT 生成完成（6/50）。
registry: pilot_011 RUNNING；其他全 DONE。

### 4. 📋 待办
- pilot_011 cron 回调 → spawn worker 跑 probe + GC4 计算
- GC4 ≥2pp → 转架构 narrative；<2pp → null-result paper outline 已就绪

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
Falcon-H1R 是最后一个数据点。已知 4B/9B GC4 都 FAIL。
易错：不要把 GC4-par (Falcon parallel) 和 GC4-seq (Qwen sequential) 直接对比。

- **§4 L43-44 修复 worker 完成** → 验证 tightness 段已改为双因素叙事（范围 45–90% / 94–99% + RV baseline 归因）。当前 ROADMAP 错误标记为 ✅。
- **crossmodel L97 E-F1 符号修复 worker 完成** → 验证 "+0.03" 已改为 "-0.03"（Qwen SciERC E-F1: 31.01→30.98）。
- **Llama bootstrap CI worker 完成** → 审查 4 个比较的 p-value，特别关注 E-F1 -0.64pp 是否显著——若显著需修改 "F1-neutral" 措辞。
- **Dream 结果注入** → 逐条审查 action items，评估是否有新的 critical 问题。
- **最终全文审查请求** → Mode B 全文写作审查（8 页压缩版），出评分模板。

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
**核心叙事**：OntoCoDec 两层约束解码，grammar 消除 RV/ET，semantic 减 DR 45–99%，F1 preserved。CF trade-off 是已知 limitation。

**决策链关键节点**：
- D014：tightness scaling 从单因素改双因素（tightness × RV baseline）。Llama DocRED DR↓90.2% vs Qwen 45% 在同一 ontology 上打破了纯 tightness 归因。**§4 是唯一未同步的文件。**
- D013：post-hoc baseline 不以 F1 论高低（post-hoc F1 更高但丢 TP）。
- D007：S2 降级为 appendix，S1 为主推策略。

**易错点**：
1. crossmodel L92 Qwen DocRED R-F1 写 "+0.49pp"（表格减法），其他文件统一写 "+0.50pp"（bootstrap delta）——两者均可辩护但不一致，info 级。
2. "comparable" 一词：用于 baseline violation rate 可比性（15.37% vs 18.46%）时正确，用于 DR reduction 可比性（90.2% vs 45%）时错误。abstract/conclusion 已改为 "substantial"，但审新文本时需警惕回退。
3. Llama DocRED parse failures 91 vs 68（+34%）是已知 limitation，文中已归因于 max-token truncation。F1 仅在成功解析文档上计算——这是 selection bias 但已在 limitations 中透明披露。
4. Registry 中 Llama SciERC 的 CF_rate 存储为 0.0656（6.56%），但 CF 原始 count=6，violation_report_v2 中 CF_rate=0.0024（0.24%）才是 unconstrained 的正确值。表格已修正为 0.24%。注意区分 unconstrained CF（0.24%）和 full_s1 CF（6.56%）。
